## CYCLE 65 (2026-07-21) — ITEM 1 ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED + FIX. Live investigation (curl against production API + read of `scripts/manage_loans_sql.js` / `pages/manage_loans.html`) resolved cycle-64 item 1 to root cause **(a) filter/information-architecture defect** — NOT a click bug, NOT a data-state issue. The "Active Loans" tab is CONFIRMED CORRECT against live data (LN-0001, status `approved`, genuinely appears). The real defect: two of the 7 tab labels ("Approved (Awaiting Disbursement)", "Disbursed Loans") describe a disbursement distinction that **cannot exist on this system** — `loans.approve` prices, schedules, and effectively disburses in one atomic call, so no loan ever sits "awaiting disbursement" and no endpoint ever sets status `disbursed`. A user clicks those reasonable-sounding tabs, sees nothing, and concludes "the filter is broken," when the correct tab (Active) sits right beside them and works. Traces to the **C5/C6** ported-page surface; no checklist box reopens; cycles not renumbered.

### Verified facts (this session — live API + grep-confirmed, not assumed)
- **Reachable loan statuses only** (confirmed by the code that SETs each): `pending` (loans.request), `approved` (loans.php:442 — this loan is now active/disbursed in practice), `rejected` (loans.php:532), `completed` (repayments.php:560, set when remainingBalance hits 0). **`disbursed` is a dead ENUM value — no endpoint ever assigns it.** **`defaulted` is ALSO dead** — grep of `api/handlers/loans.php` + `repayments.php` shows NO `UPDATE loans SET status='defaulted'`; it appears only in `exports.php:39` as a member of an export inclusion *list*, never assigned. So the system produces exactly 4 statuses.
- **`loans.list` (list_loans, loans.php:156-162) returns per loan:** `status, repaymentPeriod, approvedAt, remainingBalance, completedAt` (among others) — but **NO `dueDate` / schedule at the list level.** Per-installment `dueDate` is only returned by `loans.get` (loans.php:223-232) and `repayments.balance`, one loan at a time. So a per-installment "overdue" cannot be computed in `renderLoans()` from data the page already holds — BUT a coarse maturity-based overdue CAN: `approvedAt + repaymentPeriod months < now` AND `remainingBalance > 0` uses only fields already in the list rows. This needs NO backend change and NO extra fetch.
- Current switch (`scripts/manage_loans_sql.js:485-515`): `pending`→status==='pending' (works); `approved`→hardcoded `[]` (dead); `disbursed`→status==='disbursed' (never reachable, always empty); `active`→status IN(approved,disbursed) (CONFIRMED WORKING); `repaid`→status==='completed'; `cancelled`→status==='rejected'; `overdue`→hardcoded `[]` (dead). Existing helper `numberOf(loan.remainingBalance)` (used :461/:550/:817) already reads balance as a number for threshold checks.

### Owner decision (verbatim)
Offered (a) remove the 2 dead tabs or (b) gray them out, owner chose NEITHER: **"These should show what they are meant to and able to filter."** → REDEFINE the tabs so every one maps to a status/slice this system can actually produce.

### RECOMMENDED TAB SET (7 → 5; every tab reachable, labels non-overlapping)
| Label | data-tab | Filter predicate | Reachable via |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pending Requests | `pending` | `status === 'pending'` | loans.request |
| Active Loans | `active` | `status === 'approved' \|\| status === 'disbursed'` (keep OR — harmless if `disbursed` ever wired later; keeps parity with server `summary.activePrincipal` which counts both) | loans.approve |
| Overdue | `overdue` | `(status==='approved' \|\| status==='disbursed')` **AND** `numberOf(remainingBalance) > 0` **AND** maturity `approvedAt + repaymentPeriod months` is before now | computed client-side from list fields |
| Repaid | `repaid` | `status === 'completed'` | repayments.approve (balance→0) |
| Cancelled | `cancelled` | `status === 'rejected'` | loans.reject |
**REMOVED:** "Approved (Awaiting Disbursement)" (`data-tab="approved"`, case always `[]`) and "Disbursed Loans" (`data-tab="disbursed"`, status never set) — both describe a disbursement step this system does not have; their meaning is fully covered by Active. Removing them (rather than leaving two always-empty tabs) IS the "show what they are meant to and able to filter" outcome, since a tab that can never show a row is not filtering anything it is "able to."
**REDEFINED:** "Overdue" moves from a permanent `[]` stub to a real, reachable slice computed from data the page already fetches — active loans past their repayment period still carrying a balance. Overdue is a labelled *subset* of Active (superset/subset overlap is intentional and standard — Active = the full working set matching the server summary; Overdue = the attention subset). Note if owner later wants Active to EXCLUDE overdue rows, that is a one-line change — flag, don't assume.
**Reasoning this beats the alternatives:** deleting/graying the 2 dead tabs was explicitly rejected by the owner; leaving 3 tabs (approved/disbursed/active) all pointing at the identical filter would be visually redundant, not a fix. Reducing to 5 correctly-labelled tabs — each mapping to one of the 4 real statuses plus one genuinely-computable Overdue slice — makes every tab "show what it is meant to and able to filter" with zero backend work and no money math.

### CYCLE 65 — BOTH ITEMS DISPATCHED AND CLOSED
- **Item 2 (tablet nav toggle, `index.html`):** frontend-specialist added `<button class="nav-mobile-toggle">` (3-span hamburger, `aria-label`/`aria-expanded`/`aria-controls`) into `.nav-container`, wired `#navLinks` show/hide + close-on-tap-link, reconciled the orphaned `:377-394` CSS with the `:1238-1240` force-hide. ui-designer polish pass then fixed two real defects the functional pass didn't catch: hamburger→X animation math (`translateY` 7px/-7px → 12px/-12px, correcting for `display:flex` non-collapsing margins) and a genuine collision between the toggle and the fixed notification bell for signed-in users (consolidated 3 duplicated `:has()` margin rules into 1). **QA PASS** — verified the 12px math, the adjacent-sibling selector against real DOM order, the 44×44px touch target, and confirmed no JS/markup/aria scope creep. **DONE.**
- **Item 1 (manage_loans 7→5 tabs, `pages/manage_loans.html` + `scripts/manage_loans_sql.js`):** frontend-specialist removed the two structurally-dead tabs (`approved`, `disbursed`) and redefined `overdue` from a permanent `[]` stub into a real client-computed slice (`approvedAt` + `repaymentPeriod` months < now, `remainingBalance` > 0). First QA pass: markup change PASS, but found a genuine reproducible bug in the new overdue maturity math — `maturity.setMonth(maturity.getMonth() + months)` has JS's day-overflow bug (`new Date('2026-01-31')` + 1 month → March 3, not late Feb), silently excluding loans approved on the 29th–31st from the Overdue tab for that window. **Retry (one, per policy)** dispatched to frontend-specialist, scoped only to the maturity calc: replaced the raw `setMonth()` add with a day-clamped month-add (derive `daysInTargetMonth` via `new Date(y, m+1, 0).getDate()`, clamp the day to `Math.min(originalDay, daysInTargetMonth)`). **Re-verify QA PASS** — traced Jan-31-plus-1-month by hand to Feb 28 2026 (correct), confirmed no other switch case or file touched, `node --check` clean. **DONE.** *(QA also flagged, non-blocking: the retry diff still carries the earlier `case "approved"`/`case "disbursed"` removal from the same file, which is in-scope dead-code cleanup tied to the same loan-status model, not scope creep on the retry itself.)*

**CYCLE 65 CLOSED — both items DONE, QA PASS.** No checklist box reopened (PROJECT COMPLETE stands). Owner's broader "same as most admin pages" comment (cycle 64) named only `manage_loans` concretely; other admin pages' tab/filter correctness has not yet been swept end-to-end — flagged as a candidate follow-up, not assumed in scope without a next owner cycle naming it.

All three trace to the **C6** SPA/ported-page surface (owner-directed correctness on an already-`[x]` checklist — same class as cycles 55-65); no PROJECT COMPLETE WHEN box reopens; cycles not renumbered. **ONE objective dispatched this cycle: Issue 1** (app-wide, actively breaking, root-caused below). Issues 2 & 3 share one file (`settings_sql.js`) and a different root cause from Issue 1 → queued as the next cycle, NOT bundled here.

### Root cause of Issue 1 — CONFIRMED from the shipped router code this session (not assumed):
`scripts/spa-router.js`'s `navigateTo()` DOES sync `<head>` CSS (cycle 57 built it): `syncStylesheetLinks()` (:273-296) additively appends any missing `<link rel="stylesheet">` to the live `<head>`; `swapLocalStyles()` (:310-331) replaces the outgoing page's inline `<style>` block(s). **But the appended `<link>` loads ASYNCHRONOUSLY and there is NO onload gate** — `syncStylesheetLinks()` appends the link (:291-294) and `navigateTo()` immediately reveals the swapped content at `currentContent.replaceWith(newContent)` (:385). So on the FIRST SPA navigation to a page whose stylesheet is not yet in `<head>` (e.g. `settings.html`, whose `<link>` set differs from the other admin pages per cycle 57's md5 finding), the new content paints against not-yet-loaded CSS → the "flakey until reload" flash. A hard reload works because a full page load render-blocks on ALL its `<head>` stylesheets before first paint. This is EXACTLY the assumption cycle 57 got wrong — BUILD_PLAN:217 recorded "`<link>` insertion is non-blocking by nature; no special await needed"; that unawaited async is the residual defect. Inline `<style>` swap (`swapLocalStyles`) is synchronous (textContent) and applies instantly — NOT the problem; only external `<link>` is.

### The fix (localized to spa-router.js): gate the content reveal on the NEWLY-APPENDED stylesheets' load
`syncStylesheetLinks()` must return the set of `<link>` elements it newly appended (only the genuinely-new ones — never wait on stylesheets already present, so repeat navigation stays instant). `navigateTo()` must, after the CSS sync and BEFORE `currentContent.replaceWith(newContent)` (:385), await those new links' `load`/`error` events — with a bounded per-navigation timeout fallback (e.g. ~1500-2000ms) so a slow or failing stylesheet can NEVER hang navigation. Zero new links (the common repeat-nav case) → the await resolves immediately, no added latency. This makes a swapped-in page render styled-on-arrival on the very first visit, no reload.

## CYCLE 56 (2026-07-18) — SUPERSEDING OWNER ARCHITECTURE DECISION (not a bug, not a checklist deliverable). Convert the whole authenticated app from full-page-reload navigation to a **true SPA** (persistent nav shell + content-swap). Owner directive: *"No need to reload the side panel too when switching pages. It should be SPA."* Chosen approach (owner picked when asked): (1) persistent shell + content-swap — one shell holds sidebar/nav permanently, a nav click fetches only the target page's content, swaps it into the content area, updates the URL via History API, and re-runs that page's init/session-check; (2) whole app at once — all ~25 live pages (13 admin + 7 user + 5 public/auth per cycle-55 inventory). Same treatment as CYCLE 53 (select_group removal) and CYCLE 55 (UI/UX audit): an explicit owner directive layered on top of an already-`[x]` PROJECT COMPLETE WHEN. Cycles are not renumbered. **This REVERSES the C6 rationale** recorded in the "Migration mechanics" note ("Full-page `<a href>` nav, not an SPA content-swap (every `_sql.js` port re-runs `requireSession()` on its own `DOMContentLoaded`, which an SPA swap would skip)") — that constraint is now the exact thing this cycle re-engineers away, page by page.

### Fresh verification done this cycle (not assumed from memory)
- **The self-run pattern is universal.** Confirmed by direct grep: `scripts/user_dashboard_sql.js:51`, `scripts/manage_loans_sql.js:75`, `scripts/login_sql.js:20`, `scripts/messages_sql.js:81` each drive their entire page via `document.addEventListener("DOMContentLoaded", async () => { ... })` and export no `init`. `requireSession()` (`scripts/api.js:199`) is the first call inside those handlers.
- **Nav injection point.** Each page's inline `<script type="module">` calls `initNav({variant, activePage, pageTitle})` (`scripts/nav_sql.js:838`). `initNav` builds the sidebar as a **sibling before** `#mainContent` (`nav_sql.js:677`/`:680`) and, for admin, inserts the **topbar as the first child INSIDE `#mainContent`** (`nav_sql.js:740`). Mobile nav + toast container are appended to `<body>`.
- **Swappable region = `.dashboard-content`, NOT `#mainContent`.** Page body is `<main class="main-content" id="mainContent"><div class="dashboard-content">…page content…</div></main>` (confirmed `pages/manage_loans.html:221-222`). Swapping `#mainContent` would destroy the injected topbar that lives inside it; swapping only its inner `.dashboard-content` preserves topbar + sidebar + mobile nav (zero flicker — exactly the owner ask).

### HARD CONSTRAINT shaping the whole plan (given, not re-derived)
ES modules evaluate **exactly once per specifier** in the browser module cache, and `DOMContentLoaded` does **not** re-fire on an in-place content swap. Therefore re-inserting or re-`import()`-ing a page's `_sql.js` after a swap will NOT re-run its top-level code. So **every `_sql.js` entry point must be restructured**: the `DOMContentLoaded` body becomes an exported `init()` the router calls explicitly after each swap (and on first load). A router alone does nothing until this refactor lands, page by page.

### ARCHITECTURE DECISION (answers to the six design questions)

**1. Shell structure — NO new shell file; the initially-loaded authenticated page IS the shell.** There is no single shell HTML today and creating one would mean either double-rendering nav or rewriting all 25 files' `<body>`. Instead, whatever full page the browser loads first (via the post-login group-aware redirect from CYCLE 53 → `admin_dashboard.html` or `user_dashboard.html`) becomes the persistent shell: its `initNav`-built sidebar/topbar/mobile-nav stay in the DOM permanently, and the router only ever swaps that page's `.dashboard-content`. **Admin and user keep their structurally different navs naturally** — the first full load establishes the correct variant. **Crossing the admin↔user boundary (the "switch to member view" toggle) stays a HARD navigation**, because it changes the nav variant/structure; the SPA only swaps content *within* one variant. This gives one code path, no new file, no double nav.

**2. Content-fetch-and-swap mechanics (pjax-style).** On an intercepted nav: `fetch(targetUrl, {credentials:"same-origin"})` → parse the returned HTML with `DOMParser` into a detached document → extract `newDoc.querySelector(".dashboard-content")` → replace the live `.dashboard-content` node's contents (or the node itself) with the imported fresh node. **Never touch `<head>`, the sidebar, the topbar, the mobile nav, or `#mainContent` itself.** Nav is NOT rebuilt on swap; instead the router calls a NEW lightweight exported `updateActiveNav(activePage, pageTitle)` in `nav_sql.js` that only (a) moves the active-item highlight and (b) sets the topbar title — a partial update, never a full `initNav` re-run (a full re-run would recreate the sidebar DOM and reintroduce the exact flicker the owner wants gone). Deep-linking/refresh needs **no server rewrite** because `pushState` pushes the page's REAL file path (`pages/manage_loans.html`), which the static host still serves directly.

**3. Router module — new `scripts/spa-router.js`.** Responsibilities: (a) intercept `click` on same-app `<a>` links; (b) `history.pushState(state, "", href)`; (c) fetch-parse-swap `.dashboard-content`; (d) `updateActiveNav()`; (e) dynamic `import()` the target page's `_sql.js` (cached module returns without re-running top-level — expected; the real work is in its exported `init()`) and call `mod.init()`; (f) handle `popstate` by re-running fetch-swap-init for `location.pathname`; (g) on first load, `history.replaceState` the current URL and call the current page's `init()`. **A route map** (page filename → `{modulePath, activePage, pageTitle, variant}`) lives in `spa-router.js` and doubles as the **converted-pages whitelist** (see §rollout). **Links the router MUST NOT intercept (let the browser handle normally):** different-origin URLs; `mailto:`/`tel:`; `href` starting with `#`; any `<a download>` or export target (e.g. `exports.payments`, `exports.csv`, anything hitting `api/`); `target="_blank"` or `rel` opening a new tab; the admin↔user "switch view" toggle; login/logout transitions; and **any page not yet in the converted whitelist** (those stay hard `<a href>` navigations so the app never breaks mid-rollout).

**4. Per-page migration pattern (copyable, applied file-by-file).** For each `_sql.js`:
```
// BEFORE:
document.addEventListener("DOMContentLoaded", async () => { /* …body… */ });
// AFTER:
export async function init() { /* …exact same body… */ }
// Guarded standalone bootstrap — runs only on a real full-page load,
// never on an SPA swap. window.__bnSpa is set synchronously by the router
// during module eval, before DOMContentLoaded fires, so a converted page
// loaded under the router skips this and the router calls init() instead.
if (!window.__bnSpa) {
  document.addEventListener("DOMContentLoaded", () => { init(); });
}
```
**Idempotency is mostly a non-issue by construction:** every swap REPLACES `.dashboard-content` with a freshly parsed node, so the outgoing page's DOM (and every listener `init()` attached to it) is destroyed; A→B→A re-fetches and re-parses A's content fresh, so `init()` attaching listeners to those fresh nodes can never double-bind. **The ONLY real concern is module- or `document`/`window`-level side effects that outlive the swap** — `setInterval` polling (e.g. notifications), and listeners on `document`/`window`. A page that starts such a thing must export an optional `teardown()`; the router calls the OUTGOING page's `teardown()` (if present) before swapping in the next. Most pages need no `teardown`.

**5. Session/auth preserved — no special handling needed.** Every `init()` still calls `requireSession()` first (unchanged), so the session is re-verified on every swap. On a dead session `requireSession()` does `window.location.replace(LOGIN_URL)` (`api.js:199-206`) — a hard redirect is correct here: an expired user is being ejected entirely, not SPA-transitioned. Confirmed: no router-specific session logic required.

**6. Router scope — only nav BETWEEN already-authenticated pages.** The login→first-dashboard entry stays a HARD navigation (session/cookie doesn't exist pre-login, shell/router isn't initialized yet; CYCLE 53 already built the group-aware post-login redirect). Logout stays a hard navigation. The router bootstraps from inside `initNav` (called once per full page load) so it comes up on whatever authenticated page loaded first and never on the public/auth pages.

### ROLLOUT — staged across multiple cycles (largest change in project history; cannot be one dispatch)
- **Cycle A (THIS cycle) — build infra + prove it on a 3-page pilot.** Create `scripts/spa-router.js`, add `updateActiveNav()` to `nav_sql.js`, wire router bootstrap into `initNav`, and convert exactly 3 pilot pages' entry points + inline scripts: `admin_dashboard`, `manage_members`, `manage_loans` (the most-hopped-between admin pages, all one nav variant — ideal to validate in-variant swaps). Whitelist contains only these 3; all other links stay hard nav. COMPLEXITY: high (touches the app-wide auth/session gate pattern + History API; sonnet tier, thorough QA). Dispatch brief below.
- **Cycle B — SCOUT GATE DONE.** 10 of 13 admin pages already match the pilot's exact markup contract (`<main id="mainContent"><div class="dashboard-content">`) — ready for entry-point conversion as-is: analytics, approve_registrations, broadcast_notifications, contributions_overview, financial_reports, interest_penalties, manage_payments, manage_rules, seed_money_overview, settings. **All 7 user pages FAIL the contract** — `<main class="page-content">` (or unmarked `main-content` with no wrapper), no `id="mainContent"`, no `.dashboard-content` div: user_dashboard, user_analytics, loan_payments, contacts, messages, view_rules (settings.html is admin-variant, already counted above). Fix is identical across all 7: rename to `class="main-content" id="mainContent"`, wrap direct children in a new `<div class="dashboard-content">`. **5 public/auth pages confirmed out of scope** (index/login/404/complete_profile/accept_invitation — none call `initNav()`, router never activates there).
  - [x] **Cycle B1 — DONE, QA PASS (first pass).** 10 ready admin pages' entry points converted (2 disjoint parallel batches), whitelist merged into `spa-router.js` PAGE_CONFIG manually (13 total pages now whitelisted). QA independently re-verified the toggle-listener check on 4 files (not just trusting specialist self-reports) — clean. **ALL 13 ADMIN PAGES NOW SPA-CONVERTED.**
  - [x] **Cycle B2a — markup fix DONE, QA PASS (first pass).** All 7 user pages (user_dashboard, user_analytics, loan_payments, contacts, messages, view_rules — settings already admin-variant/converted) restructured to `<main id="mainContent"><div class="dashboard-content">`. QA independently verified CSS-carryover property-by-property against the original `.page-content` rule for all 5 pages that needed it (user_dashboard's own local `.main-content` rule needed no carryover, confirmed a genuine no-op wrapper) — exact match, zero regression. Minor non-blocking note: user_analytics.html has one extra dead `#mainContent.main-content{margin-left:0;min-height:0}` rule (targets nothing, harmless, not worth a fix cycle).
  - [x] **Cycle B2b — DONE, QA PASS (first pass; 3 manual fixes applied by the coordinator after specialist reports, all independently re-verified by QA).** 6 user pages converted (settings.html was already admin-variant/converted). Two real bugs surfaced and fixed before QA: (1) `view_rules_sql.js` cached 14 DOM refs as module-level `const`s evaluated once — given the project's own established once-per-module-eval constraint, a second SPA visit would silently write to detached nodes; fixed by moving all 14 into a `queryDomElements()` called at the top of `init()`. (2) `user_dashboard_sql.js`'s idle-session-timeout wiring attached 4 WINDOW-level listeners (unlike every other element-scoped listener in the file) on every `init()` call — window listeners aren't discarded by the DOM-subtree swap, so repeat navigation would accumulate them unboundedly (mousemove firing very frequently); fixed with a one-time `window.__bnIdleListenersWired` guard. (3) Merging user pages into the same flat `PAGE_CONFIG` whitelist as admin pages would have let the router attempt a content-swap across the admin↔user boundary (different sidebar markup entirely) — added a `variant` field to all 19 entries and a same-variant check in `shouldIntercept()`; QA traced both cross-variant directions confirming correct fallback to hard nav, and within-variant nav confirmed still working.

**CYCLE 56 — SPA ROLLOUT SUBSTANTIALLY COMPLETE.** All 19 authenticated pages (13 admin + 6 user) now content-swap via the router; sidebar/topbar/mobile-nav never rebuild on in-variant navigation. The 5 public/auth pages remain correctly out of scope (never call `initNav()`, router never activates — matches architecture decision #6: only nav between already-authenticated pages goes through the router; login→dashboard and admin↔user crossings stay hard navigations by design). The guarded `if(!window.__bnSpa)` fallback bootstrap on every page is a permanent, intentional pattern (handles direct URL entry, bookmarks, refresh, new-tab opens) — not scaffolding to be removed once rollout is "done."
- **Final cycle — retire the hard-nav fallback** (remove the per-page `if(!window.__bnSpa)` guards' necessity / drop the whitelist gate) ONLY once ALL pages are converted. Until then, a not-yet-converted page MUST keep working via normal hard `<a href>` navigation — the whitelist gate guarantees this.

### CYCLE 56 — PILOT (Cycle A) — DONE, QA PASS (first pass, no retry).
`scripts/spa-router.js` created; `nav_sql.js` got `updateActiveNav()` + router bootstrap; `admin_dashboard_sql.js`/`manage_members_new_sql.js`/`manage_loans_sql.js` converted to exported `init()` + guarded fallback. QA traced the full click→intercept→fetch→swap→init chain end-to-end and confirmed: whitelist/exclusion logic correct (external/mailto/tel/#/download/_blank/modifier-click/non-whitelisted-page all correctly fall through to hard nav), swap target is genuinely `.dashboard-content` only across all 3 pages, `__bnSpa` load-order race is safe (static import chain guarantees it's set before any page's own module runs), first-load `init()` fires exactly once, `requireSession()` ordering unweakened. One real-but-harmless finding: `admin_dashboard_sql.js`'s `setupEventListeners()` re-attaches document-level listeners on every `init()` call (accumulates on repeat navigation) — assessed the actual handler bodies and both are idempotent (no toggle state), so no user-visible bug in THIS diff, but flagged as a pattern to avoid in future page conversions (Cycle B specialists should namespace/dedupe document-level listeners, or the router should call a page's `teardown()` to remove them before swap-out).

Reason this pilot beats alternatives: proving the fetch-swap + exported-`init()` + whitelist-fallback pattern on 3 real same-variant pages de-risks the app-wide entry-point refactor before ~22 more files are touched; a router without the per-page `init()` refactor would be inert, and converting all 25 at once with an unproven pattern is the highest-blast-radius option in the project.

## CYCLE 57 (2026-07-20) — THREE OWNER-REPORTED POST-SPA BUGS, all root-caused this session against the actual current files (fresh investigation, not assumed). Issue 1 is a genuine gap in cycle 56's shipped SPA architecture (traces to the CYCLE 56 owner SPA directive — the `<head>`-CSS half of content-swap that the pilot/rollout never implemented). Issues 2 & 3 are pre-existing latent `settings.html` bugs unrelated to the SPA work (trace to **C6**/settings surface — display + tab wiring that never worked). No PROJECT COMPLETE WHEN box changes state; these harden already-`[x]` surfaces. Cycles are not renumbered.

### CYCLE 57 — ISSUE 1 (most severe): SPA navigation never syncs `<head>` CSS → pages render with wrong/missing page-specific styling until a hard reload.
**Root cause (verified):** `scripts/spa-router.js`'s `navigateTo()` swaps ONLY `.dashboard-content` and never touches `<head>`. But nearly every one of the 19 SPA-converted pages carries its OWN page-local `<style>` block(s) in `<head>`/before `<body>` (admin_dashboard: 2, most other admin pages: 1, user_dashboard: 2, other user pages: 1 each) — NOT inside `.dashboard-content`. After a swap, page B's content renders against page A's stale local `<style>` (or with B's local style absent) until a hard reload. Additionally, `settings.html`'s `<link rel="stylesheet">` set differs from the other admin pages (md5 of its extracted `<link>` block `606d7e723cfc64e3b8dd042ae16224f6` vs the 12 other admin pages' shared `ec1accbf5948ae9669fb2b26671b6518`; `admin_dashboard.html` distinct again at `31f63bdebb103c5a64d8c6fd8b6d1f17`), so navigating TO settings via the router leaves it missing whatever stylesheet(s) the previous admin page didn't already load.
**Required fix (`navigateTo()` must, per navigation, sync `<head>` CSS between the live doc and the freshly-fetched target doc):**
1. **`<link rel="stylesheet">` — ADDITIVE ONLY.** Add any stylesheet `<link>` present in the target doc's `<head>` but absent from the live `<head>` (create + insert a real `<link>` element). NEVER remove a `<link>` — a stylesheet another page still relies on must survive a swap-back; a superfluous already-loaded sheet does no harm. `<link>` insertion is non-blocking by nature; no special await needed.
2. **Page-local `<style>` blocks — REPLACE, not accumulate.** A page-local `<style>` is page-specific content, not a reusable resource. Track which `<style>` element(s) belong to "the current page" (e.g. tag them `data-spa-page-style` on first SPA-managed navigation, capturing/removing any pre-existing local `<style>` and re-inserting fresh per page) and REPLACE them with the target page's local `<style>` on every swap. Not append: two pages' local blocks can define conflicting rules for the same selector and accumulating them makes cascade order DOM-order-dependent and unpredictable.
3. **Do NOT touch shared/global stylesheets** (design-system.css, admin-layout.css, unified-navigation.css, unified-mobile-nav.css, pages.css, login.css) beyond the additive `<link>` rule in point 1 — they are identical across same-variant pages already. The real bug is page-LOCAL `<style>` blocks + any page-specific `<link>` not already loaded.
- **COMPLEXITY: high** — correction to shipped, multiply-QA-passed infra (`spa-router.js`) that all 19 converted pages depend on; same blast radius as the original router. Sonnet tier, thorough QA — trace against a concrete admin_dashboard → settings scenario (confirmed DIFFERENT stylesheet sets AND settings has its own local `<style>`): confirm the missing `<link>` is ADDED and the local `<style>` is REPLACED (not appended alongside the stale one). Dispatch brief below (dispatch 1).

### CYCLE 57 — ISSUE 2 + ISSUE 3: `pages/settings.html` display + tabs broken by ID mismatches / missing handler in `scripts/settings_sql.js`. Pre-existing, NOT SPA-related. Both live in the SAME file → ONE combined dispatch (dispatch 2). Standard tier (single file, no money math, no security surface).
**ISSUE 2 — ID mismatches (settings_sql.js targets ids that don't exist in the live markup):**
- `renderAvatar()` (settings_sql.js:103-106) looks for `profileImagePreview`/`profileAvatar` — NEITHER exists. Real markup (settings.html:265-267): `<div class="profile-picture" id="profilePicture"><span id="profileInitials">U</span><img id="profileImage" src="" style="display:none"></div>`. Fix: target `profileImage` (the `<img>` — toggle its `display`, set `.src`) and `profileInitials` (the `<span>` fallback); restructure the logic — two separate elements (img + span) shown/hidden by whether a photo exists, not one container.
- Email line (settings_sql.js:90) does `setText("userEmail", …)` — no `userEmail` element exists. Real markup (settings.html:278): `<div class="profile-email" id="profileEmail">`. This IS the owner's "email below the avatar" bug: nothing is written to the real element, so placeholder "email@example.com" persists. Fix: target `profileEmail`.
- Photo-upload wiring (settings_sql.js:51-56) attaches to `profileImageInput` (change) and `changePhotoBtn` (click) — NEITHER exists. Real markup (settings.html:269,275): `<label for="profilePictureInput" class="profile-picture-edit">…</label><input type="file" id="profilePictureInput" …>`. The `<label for=>` already opens the picker natively, so the `changePhotoBtn` click-to-open wiring can be REMOVED; only the `change` listener needs rewiring to `profilePictureInput` so `uploadProfileImage()` actually fires.
- Name display already works (`profileName` exists at settings.html:277; `displayName` is a harmless dead fallback) — leave it.
- Fix `scripts/settings_sql.js` only — the HTML ids are the live, correct markup (other code already targets `profileName` etc.).
**ISSUE 3 — tab buttons have NO click handler anywhere (grep: zero `settings-tab`/`data-panel` in settings_sql.js, zero in any `<script>` in settings.html; only modal-utils.js, dob-validation.js, settings_sql.js load, none contain tab logic):**
- Markup contract: settings.html:283-286 `<button class="settings-tab active" data-panel="personal">` (+ professional/security/account; only personal starts `.active`). settings.html:290,360,427,482 `<div class="settings-panel active" id="personalPanel">` (+ professionalPanel/securityPanel/accountPanel; only personalPanel starts `.active`). CSS at settings.html:123-127 controls visibility via `.active` (verify exact values before writing — don't assume `display:none`).
- Fix: add a click handler (in settings_sql.js, in `setupEventListeners()` or a new fn called from `init()`) for every `.settings-tab`: on click, remove `.active` from all `.settings-tab` and all `.settings-panel`, then add `.active` to the clicked tab and to `#${tab.dataset.panel}Panel`.

### DISPATCH STRUCTURE (dispatches 1 & 2 are disjoint — different files — and MAY run as 2 concurrent parallel frontend-specialists; max-2-concurrent satisfied).

## CYCLE 59 (2026-07-20) — SYSTEMATIC APP-WIDE DEAD-SELECTOR SWEEP. Same latent-bug category as cycles 57-58 (owner keeps finding pre-existing id/class-mismatch bugs one page at a time through manual testing), now generalised beyond tabs to EVERY static DOM reference. This session alone found and fixed THREE more instances of the identical bug class — `pages/settings.html`, `pages/manage_payments.html`, `pages/manage_rules.html` each had `_sql.js` code calling `document.getElementById("someId")` / `document.querySelector(".someClass")` for an id/class that does NOT exist in that page's real static HTML, so the code silently does nothing (buttons never fire, display fields never populate, listeners never attach). NOT a new deliverable — PROJECT COMPLETE WHEN is fully `[x]`; this is the same "surface latent pre-existing bugs proactively instead of waiting for the owner to keep finding them" work as cycles 57-58. No checklist box reopens. Cycles are not renumbered.

**Rationale for doing it ONE time, systematically, now.** Each of the last three finds cost a manual side-by-side read of two files and hand cross-checking every reference. There are ~24 live page/script pairs. A single mechanical sweep across all of them is far cheaper and far more thorough than continuing to discover these one owner-test at a time — and this is exactly the kind of set-diff a script does more reliably than an LLM eyeballing ~48 files (fewer misses on the long tail of rarely-visited pages).

**Approach chosen: SCRIPTED grep-and-diff, LLM confirms candidates (hybrid).** Instruct the qa-auditor to WRITE AND RUN a throwaway node/shell script (into the scratch/temp area, NOT the repo) that, per page: (1) extracts every `id="…"` and `class="…"` token from the page's static HTML; (2) extracts every static-string-literal DOM target from the matching JS — `getElementById("…")`, `querySelector("…")`, `querySelectorAll("…")`, AND the string-literal first argument of each file's own DOM-lookup helper wrappers (these ported files use helpers like `setText("id",…)`, `setValue("id",…)`, `setHtml(…)`, `on("id",…)`, `$("…")`, `qs("…")` that wrap `getElementById`/`querySelector` — the settings bug hid behind exactly such a helper, so a raw `getElementById`-only regex would MISS it; the auditor must first identify each file's helper set and include their id-string args); (3) diffs the two sets and emits, per page, the JS-referenced selectors that match no static HTML id/class = CANDIDATES. The script gives coverage and cheapness; the LLM then confirms each candidate against the three caveats below so the final findings list is clean, not a flood of false positives.

**The full ~24-page audit set (page HTML → its script). select_group.html + group_page.html are confirmed deleted/dead → EXCLUDED.**
- 13 admin: admin_dashboard, manage_members (script `manage_members_new_sql.js`), manage_loans, analytics, financial_reports, manage_payments, manage_rules, seed_money_overview, settings, approve_registrations, broadcast_notifications, contributions_overview, interest_penalties.
- 6 user: user_dashboard, user_analytics, loan_payments, contacts, messages, view_rules.
- 5 public/auth: index, login (`login_sql.js`), 404, complete_profile, accept_invitation. (These may use inline `<script type="module">` rather than a `_sql.js` — the auditor extracts from whatever script the page actually loads, inline or external. settings.html/manage_payments.html/manage_rules.html are IN the set to re-confirm this session's three fixes landed clean, not as new suspects.)

**THREE mandatory false-positive filters (a candidate is a REAL finding only if it survives all three).** These are what separate the settings/manage_payments/manage_rules bug pattern from legitimate code:
1. **Dynamic / JS-created elements are NOT findings.** The bug pattern is: JS assumes a STATIC element already exists in the page's own markup, but it doesn't. If the flagged id/class is itself created at runtime by the SAME JS (via `createElement` + `el.id = …`/`el.className = …`/`setAttribute`, or appears only inside a template-string the JS injects as innerHTML/insertAdjacentHTML for content it renders), it is working-as-designed, NOT a finding. For every candidate, the auditor confirms the element is genuinely expected to pre-exist in static HTML before flagging it.
2. **Nav-injected shared ids/classes are NOT findings against a page's OWN static HTML.** `scripts/nav_sql.js` builds the sidebar/topbar/mobile-nav at runtime and injects ids/classes (e.g. `logoutBtn`, `mobileLogoutBtn`, and every element it creates) that pages legitimately reference but never carry in their own static markup. FIRST STEP for the auditor: grep `nav_sql.js`'s own `.id =` / `.className =` / `setAttribute("id",…)` / `setAttribute("class",…)` / `id="…"`/`class="…"` inside its template strings to build the NAV-INJECTED EXCLUSION LIST, then subtract that set from every page's candidates. A shared nav id missing from a page's static HTML is expected, not a bug.
3. **`||`-fallback / dual-candidate id patterns — finding ONLY if ALL candidates miss.** Some ported files intentionally try two ids for the same purpose (e.g. `setText("displayName", …); setText("profileName", …)` where only one id exists per page by design, or `getElementById("a") || getElementById("b")`). If AT LEAST ONE candidate in such a pair resolves to a real static element, it is working-as-designed → NOT a finding. Only flag when BOTH/ALL candidates in the fallback group are missing (that is a genuine dead reference, the real-bug case).

**PHASE 1 (this cycle) — READ-ONLY scripted sweep + confirmed candidate list. Dispatched now (brief below).** Output: a per-page list of confirmed dead static-selector references (id/class the JS expects in static HTML but that genuinely does not exist there, surviving all three filters), each with JS file:line + the missing selector + a one-line symptom (what silently breaks). Plus an explicit "no dead static selectors found" for clean pages. NO fixes this cycle — verdicts only (read-only gate).

**PHASE 2 (next cycle, AFTER Phase 1 returns — briefs NOT written yet, they depend on findings).** For each confirmed dead reference, write frontend-specialist fix briefs (retarget to the real static id/class, or add the missing static element — decided per finding by reading the real markup), following the exact cycle-57/58 pattern. Batch into disjoint single-file dispatches, max 2 concurrent. Not COMPLEXITY:high (per-page single-file UI wiring, no money math, no security surface) unless a finding lands in a money/auth path, in which case that one is flagged high.

For the group-info display: since NO preview endpoint exists, populate #groupName/#memberCount/#contribution ONLY if the redeem success response carries that data; otherwise show honest neutral copy (e.g. #groupName "You've been invited to a savings group", hide the numeric #memberCount/#contribution rows) rather than "Loading..." forever. ## CYCLE 61 (2026-07-21) — OWNER DIRECTIVE: "go through every page and confirm accounting logic and simplicity." READ-ONLY money-correctness + simplicity AUDIT across every money-touching surface. Traces to **A2** (all money computed server-side in integer minor units; no client does money math) — this is a VERIFICATION of an already-`[x]` deliverable, not a new deliverable; A2 stays checked, this re-proves it against the live code. Same class as cycles 55/56/60: an explicit owner directive layered on a complete checklist. Cycles not renumbered. **NO CODE CHANGES THIS CYCLE — findings only.** Any fix a finding warrants is a separate follow-up cycle (and, if it turns out to be a pure refactor with no behaviour change, it goes to FUTURE IDEAS pending owner go-ahead, not the active backlog).

### Scope — the owner named two dimensions; audit both:
1. **ACCOUNTING LOGIC CORRECTNESS.** Verify the app's own documented convention actually holds everywhere: money is NEVER computed client-side; the server returns canonical 2-dp strings derived from integer minor units; the client only displays/sums what the server already sent. Separately verify the SERVER-side math itself is internally consistent and matches its own documented rules (reduced-balance vs flat-rate interest, fixed-daily penalty accrual post-grace, the penalty→interest→principal split order on a repayment, seed-money/monthly-contribution/service-fee obligations, and the cycle equity/payout/settle share-out math).
2. **SIMPLICITY.** Flag accounting-adjacent code that is needlessly complex, duplicated across files instead of shared, or divergent between copies.

### Fresh verification done this cycle (grep-confirmed, not assumed):
- **Shared server money library EXISTS and is canonical:** `api/lib/money.php` — `money_to_minor()` (:32), `money_from_minor()` (:52), `money_rate_to_hundredths()` (:69), `money_div_round_half_up()` (:94, half-up integer division), `compute_loan_schedule()` (:137, the single interest/schedule builder). The audit's core backend question is whether EVERY handler routes through these and none does ad-hoc `round()`/`number_format()`/float money math of its own.
- **The percentage-contribution-penalty refusal is STILL in place:** `api/handlers/payments.php:188` still `throw new RuntimeException('Percentage contribution penalties are not implemented.')` inside `compute_contribution_penalty()` (:154). Cycle 49's P5 finding stands; the audit re-confirms nothing since has silently implemented a guessed formula. This remains BLOCKED on the owner supplying the formula (basis/period/compounding) — unchanged, still parked, not re-queued.
- **CONFIRMED simplicity finding (already established this session):** the module CLAUDE.md names as the single home for ALL currency math — `scripts/utils_financial.js` — **DOES NOT EXIST anywhere in the repo.** Every page-script defines its own local `formatCurrency`/`formatCurrencyFromMinor`. Audit B must document the EXTENT: which files, and whether the copies are byte-identical (pure duplication) or divergent (a differing decimal/rounding/symbol/separator rule between two files is a real inconsistency bug source, not just tidiness).
- **All target handlers confirmed to exist:** `api/handlers/loans.php`, `repayments.php`, `payments.php`, `cycle.php` (+ `api/lib/money.php`).

### Money-touching surfaces in scope (read-only this pass):
- **Backend (server = source of truth):** `api/lib/money.php` (shared helpers) · `api/handlers/loans.php` (interest calc, approve/reject, force-loan) · `api/handlers/repayments.php` (record/approve/reject/waive; penalty+interest+principal split order) · `api/handlers/payments.php` (obligations; seed/monthly/service-fee; fixed-daily penalty accrual; the percentage refusal at :188) · `api/handlers/cycle.php` (equity/payout/settle/forced-loans preview — share-out math).
- **Frontend money DISPLAY:** the `_sql.js` scripts behind manage_loans, loan_payments, manage_payments, contributions_overview, seed_money_overview, interest_penalties, financial_reports, analytics, user_analytics, admin_dashboard, user_dashboard, manage_rules — plus a repo-wide grep for every `formatCurrency`-family definition.

### DISPATCH PLAN — 2 qa-auditor dispatches, both READ-ONLY, both COMPLEXITY: high (verifying real financial correctness; escalated tier warranted even though nothing is written). Audit C (percentage-penalty re-confirmation) is folded into Audit A item 4 — it is a re-check, not new work. Run file-disjoint, may be concurrent (Audit A = api/, Audit B = scripts/).

### CYCLE 61 — RESULTS

**AUDIT A (backend) — PASS. AD-HOC MONEY MATH: 0 · RULE MISMATCHES: 0 · PERCENTAGE REFUSAL INTACT: yes.** All 5 files route every conversion/rounding through `api/lib/money.php`. `compute_loan_schedule()` is the sole interest source (loans.php:401,630 — no duplicate math elsewhere); `reduced_balance` implemented, `flat_rate` correctly 501-refused (not silently approximated). Repayment split confirmed penalty→interest→principal (repayments.php:146-228,412-616), penalty tracked separately and excluded from amountRepaid/remainingBalance by design. Contribution penalty grace+fixed-daily logic (payments.php:216-230) is direct integer multiplication, not a rounding shortcut — not a defect. Percentage-penalty refusal re-confirmed intact (payments.php:188, unchanged since cycle 49's P5). cycle.php's payout/settle math has a HARD reconciliation gate that throws before returning/writing anything unbalanced (cycle.php:362-366,706-709) — self-checking, not just self-consistent.
**One scope gap noted, not a finding:** `api/lib/penalty.php` (loan-penalty engine) wasn't in the audited 5 files; payments.php:125's comment claims it's "the same engine shape" as the contribution-penalty logic, but that claim is unverified. Follow-up recommended if a from-scratch comparison matters.

**AUDIT B (frontend) — FAIL. This is the real finding from "confirm accounting logic and simplicity."** Two distinct, both genuine, problems:
1. **Formatter chaos — 13 files, no canonical formatter, confirmed DIVERGENT.** `scripts/utils_financial.js` (the module CLAUDE.md names as the single home for currency math) still does not exist. 9 of 13 files split cleanly into Group-A (0-decimal `toLocaleString`) vs Group-B (2-decimal `toLocaleString`) — SAME kind of MWK amount renders as `"MWK 1,234"` on analytics.html but `"MWK 1,234.00"` on contributions_overview.html. 4 files are structurally different implementations: `admin_dashboard_sql.js` does its own minor-units math with hand-rolled regex grouping (always 0dp); `user_dashboard_sql.js`'s `formatMoney` TRUNCATES the fractional part instead of rounding (`"12.999"` → `"12.99"`, everywhere else would round to `"13.00"` — a genuine rounding-direction divergence, not just cosmetic); `view_rules_sql.js` has no fixed decimal count at all (varies 0-3 digits per value); `currency-selector.js` is a separate multi-currency implementation.
2. **26 confirmed instances of client-side money aggregation** across admin_dashboard/analytics/financial_reports/interest_penalties/manage_loans/manage_payments/user_analytics/user_dashboard — dashboards and reports sum raw per-record fields (amountPaid, arrears, penalty.amountAccrued, principalAmount, remainingBalance) client-side into totals, rather than displaying a server-precomputed sum. This is NOT re-deriving interest/penalty formulas (that stays server-side, confirmed clean in Audit A) — it's addition/subtraction of already-correct individual values. The real risk is JS floating-point addition of decimal money values, not conceptual wrongness of what's being summed. `analytics_sql.js:280`'s `interestPaid` is the one confirmed exception — explicitly sourced from a server-aggregated field, not re-summed — but it's then added to a client-summed `collected` figure anyway, so the combined total is still client-derived.

**LOCAL FORMATTERS: 13 files (4 structurally divergent + 9 split 0dp/2dp) · CLIENT-SIDE MONEY ARITHMETIC: 26 call sites.**

**Decision needed before any fix cycle:** remediation is nontrivial architecture work (create a real `scripts/utils_financial.js` and either move all 26 aggregations server-side as new/extended API responses, or standardize the client-side summation to a single safe minor-units helper) — this is a genuine simplicity/consistency debt, not a one-line patch, and wasn't authorized as write-work by the read-only audit directive. **AWAITING OWNER: how to proceed on the 2 confirmed findings** — (a) formatter consolidation into a real utils_financial.js (mechanical, lower risk), (b) client-side aggregation — server-side pre-summing (bigger, safer) vs. a shared safe-summation helper (smaller, still client-side).

## CYCLE 62 (2026-07-21) — REMEDIATE cycle 61's Audit B findings. OWNER DECISION (explicit, this session): move the 26 client-side money aggregations to the SERVER (pre-summed API fields), NOT a shared client-side summation helper. Formatter consolidation resolved by extension: once totals arrive pre-computed, the client's remaining job is pure DISPLAY, so `scripts/utils_financial.js` becomes a small display-only module. Traces to **A2** (all money computed server-side in integer minor units; no client does money math) — hardening an already-`[x]` deliverable against the two confirmed cycle-61 defects; A2 stays `[x]`. Same owner-directed class as cycles 55/60. Cycles not renumbered. **COMPLEXITY: high throughout — this app moves real money; every backend dispatch is sonnet-tier with thorough per-dispatch QA.**

### Endpoint grouping of the 26 call sites (derived from cycle-61 Audit B fields + confirmed against the live handlers this cycle — `payments.list`/`loans.list` confirmed as admin_dashboard's sources at admin_dashboard_sql.js:148-149; `list_payments()` row-build confirmed at payments.php:538-588):
The 8 affected files sum from **three main endpoints + one cycle preview**, so this is ~4 backend changes, not 26:
- **`payments.list`** (returns `payments[]`; fields summed client-side: `amountPaid`, `arrears`, `penalty.amountAccrued`, filtered by `approvalStatus`/`paymentType`): admin_dashboard_sql.js:263-291,317-342 · analytics_sql.js:277,314 · financial_reports_sql.js:272,333,357-361 · manage_payments_sql.js:346-357. **(Stage 1 proof-of-pattern.)**
- **`loans.list`** (returns `loans[]`; fields summed: `principalAmount ?? approvedAmount`, `remainingBalance`, `totalInterest`, filtered by `status`): admin_dashboard_sql.js:333 · analytics_sql.js:287,329,383,396 · financial_reports_sql.js:275,279,285 · interest_penalties_sql.js:373 · manage_loans_sql.js:447-466 · user_analytics_sql.js:198-201.
- **`payments.obligations`** (returns per-member obligation with `seedMoney`/`months[]`/`serviceFee`, each carrying `amountPaid`/`arrears`/`penalty.amountAccrued`): user_dashboard_sql.js:445-492 · user_analytics_sql.js:186-194 · manage_payments_sql.js:672.
- **`cycle.forced.preview`** (returns `members[]` each with `shortfall`): manage_loans_sql.js:1110.

### DESIGN — backend summary fields (each accumulated in integer minor units via api/lib/money.php `money_to_minor`/`money_from_minor`; NO new float math, NO new query — accumulate inside the handler's existing row loop):
- **`payments.list` → new `summary` object** (Stage 1, briefed below): `{verifiedCollected, arrears, penaltyAccrued, totalArrears}`, all canonical 2dp strings from `money_from_minor`. `verifiedCollected` = Σ amountPaid where approvalStatus ∈ {approved,completed}; `arrears` = Σ per-row arrears; `penaltyAccrued` = Σ per-row `penalty.amountAccrued` (reusing the already-built `$row['penalty']`); `totalArrears` = arrears+penaltyAccrued (exactly admin_dashboard's tile).
- **`loans.list` → new `summary` object** (Stage 2 Wave A): `{totalPrincipal, totalOutstanding, totalInterest, activePrincipal}` — Σ principalAmount, Σ remainingBalance, Σ totalInterest, and Σ principal where status active — mirrors the same accumulate-in-loop pattern. Backend-specialist confirms the loans.list row loop before the brief is written.
- **`payments.obligations` → new `summary`** (Stage 2 Wave B): `{contributed, arrears, penaltyAccrued}` per-member totals across seedMoney+months+serviceFee.
- **`cycle.forced.preview` → new `totalShortfall`** (Stage 2 Wave C): Σ member shortfall.

### DESIGN — frontend (mechanical, per endpoint, only AFTER that endpoint's backend field is live + QA-passed — server-then-client, never all-at-once): each of the 8 files stops calling `.reduce`/`+=` on the raw rows, reads the new server-provided total, and deletes the now-dead accumulation. Not dispatched until the paired backend field passes QA.

### DESIGN — `scripts/utils_financial.js` (Stage 1, briefed below; disjoint from all other work, no dependency, parallel-safe). Canonical rule: **2 decimal places** (recommended and adopted). Reasoning: (1) the server already emits canonical 2dp strings from `money_from_minor`, so 2dp display matches the source of truth losslessly; (2) a savings/loan app reconciles seed money, contributions, interest and penalties to the cent — dropping cents (the 0dp group) hides real balances; (3) standardizing UP to 2dp resolves the "MWK 1,234" vs "MWK 1,234.00" divergence in one direction and lets the module replace user_dashboard's TRUNCATING `formatMoney` with a rounding formatter, killing the one real rounding-direction bug. Display-only: no summation, no rounding of raw floats beyond fixed-2dp display. Single import for all 13 files at the final stage.

### ROLLOUT (staged, max 2 concurrent per wave, server-then-client per endpoint):
- **Stage 1 (dispatched now, 2 concurrent, disjoint files):** (a) create `scripts/utils_financial.js`; (b) add the `summary` object to `payments.list` (proof-of-pattern — highest-fanout money endpoint AND the admin dashboard's collections/arrears tiles, first thing an admin sees).

### CYCLE 62 — STAGE 1 DISPATCH BRIEF — A (create the display-only formatter module)
```
AGENT      frontend-specialist
COMPLEXITY high — a money-formatting module used app-wide; a wrong decimal/rounding rule misstates real money on every page.
OBJECTIVE  Create scripts/utils_financial.js as the single DISPLAY-ONLY currency formatter for the app: canonical 2dp MWK formatting, no arithmetic, no aggregation. Traces to A2.
FILES      scripts/utils_financial.js — NEW FILE, create it. Touch nothing else.
CONTEXT    - The app moves real money; server endpoints already return canonical 2-dp strings from money_from_minor() (e.g. "1234.00"). The client's only remaining job is DISPLAY: comma thousands grouping + "MWK " prefix + fixed 2 decimals.
           - 13 files currently carry divergent local formatters (cycle 61 Audit B): a 0dp group ("MWK 1,234") vs a 2dp group ("MWK 1,234.00") for the same amount, and user_dashboard's formatMoney TRUNCATES the fraction ("12.999"->"12.99") instead of rounding — a real bug this module must NOT reproduce.
           - Plain ES modules, no build step; other scripts will import via `import { formatCurrency } from "./utils_financial.js"`.
           - Two input shapes exist app-wide: numeric/2dp-string amounts (most files call formatCurrency(number)) and integer minor units (admin_dashboard/user_dashboard use formatCurrencyFromMinor(minor)). Support BOTH so every one of the 13 files can migrate later without changing its data source.
DO NOT     - Do NOT add any sum/multiply/divide/subtract or rounding-of-raw-floats logic — display only.
           - Do NOT import from or modify any other file; do NOT touch currency-selector.js (multi-currency is out of scope for this module).
           - Do NOT truncate fractional parts (no String().slice, no bitwise | 0) — use toFixed/Intl so values ROUND, never truncate.
           - Do NOT use a locale that drops grouping; use a fixed-grouping locale.
ACCEPT     - Exports EXACTLY two functions:
             `export function formatCurrency(value)` — value is a number or numeric string; returns "MWK 1,234.00" (always 2dp, comma grouping, "MWK " prefix). NaN/non-finite/empty -> "MWK 0.00".
             `export function formatCurrencyFromMinor(minorUnits)` — integer count of minor units (1/100 MWK); returns the same "MWK 1,234.00" format (divide by 100 for display only).
           - Formatting via Intl.NumberFormat('en-US', {minimumFractionDigits:2, maximumFractionDigits:2}) or equivalent fixed-grouping — never truncation.
           - Header comment states: single canonical DISPLAY-ONLY money formatter; all aggregation is server-side.
           - node --check scripts/utils_financial.js passes (and eslint clean if a config resolves). No other file changed — git status shows only the new file.
```

### CYCLE 62 — STAGE 1 DISPATCH BRIEF — B (payments.list server-side summary — proof of pattern)
```
AGENT      backend-specialist
COMPLEXITY high — adds a server-computed money aggregate to a live money endpoint; correctness of collections/arrears totals shown on the admin dashboard.
OBJECTIVE  Add a server-computed `summary` object to the payments.list response, pre-summing the collected/arrears/penalty figures the client currently re-adds, accumulated in integer minor units via api/lib/money.php. Traces to A2.
FILES      api/handlers/payments.php — the list_payments() function ONLY (currently lines 538-588). Do not touch any other function.
CONTEXT    - list_payments() (payments.php:538) fetches a group's payment rows; a member caller is SQL-restricted to their OWN rows at :560-563 (an authorization boundary, not a filter) — the summary must cover EXACTLY the rows returned, never wider.
           - Per row (:574-585) the handler already computes $arrearsMinor = money_to_minor($row['arrears']) and $row['penalty'] via payment_penalty_or_501(), whose returned object has amountAccrued as a 2dp string.
           - api/lib/money.php: money_to_minor() (:32), money_from_minor() (:52). ALL accumulation MUST be integer minor units; NO float, NO round(), NO number_format().
           - Verified-collected rule (from admin_dashboard_sql.js:245-266): a payment counts as collected only when approvalStatus in {approved, completed}. Each row carries $row['approvalStatus'] and $row['amountPaid'] (2dp string).
           - payment_penalty_or_501() already 501s the whole endpoint for percentage-penalty groups before any row completes, so reading amountAccrued for the summary adds NO new failure mode.
DO NOT     - Do NOT change the per-row shape, the SQL, or the WHERE/authorization clauses (the member-only :560 filter stays; the summary reflects it, never bypasses it).
           - Do NOT add float math, round(), number_format(), or a second query — accumulate inside the existing foreach at :574.
           - Do NOT recompute penalty — reuse $row['penalty']['amountAccrued'] already built at :578.
           - Do NOT touch record_payment/approve/reject/obligations or any other handler/file.
ACCEPT     - Inside list_payments()'s existing foreach, three integer-minor accumulators: collectedVerifiedMinor (+= money_to_minor($row['amountPaid']) ONLY when approvalStatus in {approved,completed}), arrearsMinor (+= the per-row $arrearsMinor already computed at :575), penaltyAccruedMinor (+= money_to_minor($row['penalty']['amountAccrued'])).
           - json_response returns, alongside the UNCHANGED 'payments' array, a new 'summary': {verifiedCollected: money_from_minor(collectedVerifiedMinor), arrears: money_from_minor(arrearsMinor), penaltyAccrued: money_from_minor(penaltyAccruedMinor), totalArrears: money_from_minor(arrearsMinor + penaltyAccruedMinor)} — all four canonical 2dp strings.
           - Existing 'payments' array unchanged (same rows, same per-row fields incl. penalty).
           - php -l api/handlers/payments.php passes. QA (diff only) verifies: no float/round/number_format introduced; accumulators are integer minor; summary is computed over the same $stmt rows and nowhere else (honors the member-only SQL restriction). git status shows only payments.php changed.
```

Reason this beats the alternatives: `payments.list` is the single highest-value proof-of-pattern — it is both the highest-fanout money endpoint (feeds 4 of the 8 files) and the source of the admin dashboard's collections/arrears tiles, the first money figures an admin sees; its `list_payments()` already computes per-row arrears+penalty in a loop, so the summary is a pure additive accumulation with zero new query and zero new failure mode, making it the safest place to prove the server-side-aggregation pattern before replicating it to loans.list/obligations. Pairing it with the disjoint, dependency-free `utils_financial.js` creation uses the full 2-concurrent budget without any file overlap, and defers every frontend removal until its backend field is QA-proven — exactly the server-then-client discipline the money-critical scope demands.

## CYCLE 63 (2026-07-21) — FINAL STAGE: migrate the 12 in-scope local `formatCurrency`-family definitions onto `scripts/utils_financial.js`. Picks up cycle 62's logged-but-not-done item (1), the lower-risk mechanical one, on owner "keep going". Traces to **A2** (single-sourced money display). A2 stays `[x]`; this consolidates it — same class as cycle 62, which created the module A2 now points at. Cycles not renumbered.

**FRESH-GREP RE-VERIFICATION (this cycle, not assumed from cycle 61's audit — cycle 62 shifted several line numbers but changed no local formatter's LOGIC).** Current definition sites:
- **Group-A (local `formatCurrency`, 0dp — `min/maxFractionDigits:0`, shows "MWK 1,234"):** `analytics_sql.js:509`, `financial_reports_sql.js:508`, `manage_loans_sql.js:1433`. Confirmed still byte-identical 0dp.
- **Group-B (local `formatCurrency`, 2dp — `min/maxFractionDigits:2`, shows "MWK 1,234.00"):** `contributions_overview_sql.js:554`, `interest_penalties_sql.js:555`, `manage_payments_sql.js:1088`, `seed_money_overview_sql.js:304`, `user_analytics_sql.js:367`, `loan_payments_sql.js:595`. Confirmed still byte-identical 2dp. **These 6 produce IDENTICAL output to the module → pure consolidation, zero visual change.**
- **Special-case (Wave 2, NOT dispatched now):** `admin_dashboard_sql.js` — already `import { formatCurrency as formatServerCurrency } from "./utils_financial.js"` (:39, partial migration from cycle 62's Stage-1b tile wiring), but STILL defines 4 locals: `formatCurrency:1558`, `formatCurrencyFromMinor:1568`, `formatCurrencyShort:1580`, `formatCurrencyShortFromMinor:1589`. Only the first two have module equivalents; the two Short variants have NO module export and must STAY. `user_dashboard_sql.js:1316` `formatMoney` — TRUNCATES via `.slice(0,2)` on the fraction (confirmed: `frac=(frac+"00").slice(0,2)`), a real rounding-direction BUG the migration also fixes. `view_rules_sql.js:48` — no fixed decimal count, varies 0-3 digits, a real inconsistency fix.
- **Out of scope, unchanged:** `currency-selector.js` (separate multi-currency implementation, deliberately excluded from utils_financial.js's design).

**THE MIGRATION MECHANIC (same-name swap — why call sites don't change).** The module exports `formatCurrency` under the EXACT name the locals use. So per file: (a) add `import { formatCurrency } from "./utils_financial.js";`, (b) DELETE the local `function formatCurrency(...)` block. A same-name `import` + `function` in one ES module is a duplicate binding (SyntaxError), so the local def MUST be removed — and once removed, every existing `formatCurrency(x)` call resolves to the import unchanged. **No call site is edited.** The Group-A 0dp→2dp shift is therefore purely a consequence of swapping the definition, and it is the INTENDED change (cycle 61's finding: the 0dp-vs-2dp divergence itself was the defect). The one risk to check: a caller that string-parses the formatted result (`.split`/`.slice`/`.replace`/`parseFloat`/`.length` applied to a `formatCurrency(...)` RETURN) — none seen in sampling, but each specialist grep-confirms before deleting a def.

**ROLLOUT (waves, max 2 concurrent, disjoint file lists). Prove the pattern on the 9 byte-identical/mechanical files FIRST, then the 3 special-cases.**
- **Wave 1 (DISPATCHED NOW — 2 concurrent, disjoint):** Dispatch 1 = Group-B (6 files, zero visual change — safest possible proof). Dispatch 2 = Group-A (3 files, intentional 0dp→2dp). Not COMPLEXITY:high — module already QA-passed, delete-def+add-import is fully mechanical, no money math authored.
- **Wave 2 (write next cycle, after Wave 1 QA-passes — flagged for extra care, NOT bundled with Wave 1):** the 3 special-case files. `user_dashboard_sql.js` PRIORITIZED (its `formatMoney`→`formatCurrency` swap also fixes the truncation bug — note `formatMoney` is a DIFFERENT name, so its call sites DO need renaming to `formatCurrency`, unlike Waves 1). `admin_dashboard_sql.js` needs reconciliation: replace only local `formatCurrency`/`formatCurrencyFromMinor` (:1558/:1568) with the already-present import (rename `formatServerCurrency` usage or import both), KEEP `formatCurrencyShort`/`formatCurrencyShortFromMinor`. `view_rules_sql.js` swap standardizes its variable-decimal output to 2dp. These get their own dispatch(es) with money-display care given the bug-fix and preserve-not-delete nuances.

No current-user profile photo anywhere in nav.** `nav_sql.js` (both `renderAdminNav` and `renderUserNav`) only ever builds initials, never an `<img>`, for the current user's own avatar. `admin_dashboard_sql.js`'s `applyOwnProfileImage()` (lines 229-251) targets `sidebarProfilePic`/`topbarProfilePic` ids that do not exist anywhere in `pages/admin_dashboard.html` or `nav_sql.js` — dead code. `contacts_sql.js` already has a working photo-render pattern for *other* members (`member.profileImageUrl` → `<img>`, lines 233-237) — reuse that pattern. `settings_sql.js`'s own `renderAvatar()` is correct and NOT in scope — it already renders the photo correctly via `profile.get`.

**Fix order:** P2 (backend, `api/lib/session.php`) + P1 (frontend, 11-file mechanical fix) in parallel — disjoint files. Then P3 (frontend: `nav_sql.js` + `admin_dashboard_sql.js`) once P2 lands, since P3's `<img>` wiring should read `profileImageUrl` off the now-fixed session payload. ui-designer polish pass on P3's new avatar markup after.

## CYCLE 49 — follow-up findings from a full page-by-page live-session sweep (curl against a local PHP server on the production DB, logging in as the same smoketest admin). P1/P2/P3 confirmed working correctly end-to-end. Two more findings:

- [x] **P4 — DONE (found already fixed in committed code, verified live by re-triggering `profile.update` and confirming `members.list` picks up the synced photo). `profile.update` never syncs `members.profileImageUrl`.** `users.profileImageUrl` and `members.profileImageUrl` are two separate denormalized columns for the same photo (per SYSTEM_MAP's documented `members` schema: fullName/email/phone/whatsappNumber/profileImageUrl are "all denormalised from users"). `profile.update` (`api/handlers/profile.php`) only writes `users`. Confirmed live: this admin's own `users.profileImageUrl` is set but their `members.profileImageUrl` row (for the group they're actually in) is null — so their own photo would show correctly wherever code reads the session/profile object (nav, P3's fix) but as blank wherever code reads `members.list` (`manage_members_new_sql.js`, `contacts_sql.js`, which already correctly render OTHER members' photos from that same column). Owner directed: fix the code (make `profile.update` sync `members.profileImageUrl` across every group row for that uid on every write), not just patch this account's test data.
- **P5 — percentage-based contribution penalties — NOT a bug, a deliberate refusal, BLOCKED on the owner.** `api/handlers/payments.php:183-188` (`compute_contribution_penalty`) explicitly throws `RuntimeException('Percentage contribution penalties are not implemented.')` rather than guess at the math — comment states plainly this app moves real money and inventing a formula would "charge against real members" incorrectly. The `[SMOKE TEST - safe to delete] Build Loop QA Group` (`eff2fcc9efb756d558c67d218d83a516`) happens to be seeded with `contributionPenaltyType='percentage'`, so `payments.obligations` 500s for that one group — breaking `contributions_overview`, `seed_money_overview`, `interest_penalties` pages for it specifically. The other 2 smoketest groups use `'fixed'` and work fine. **This is the same gap as SYSTEM_MAP Unknown #6 (loan interest formula) — a formula only the owner can supply.** Not queued as a build task. **Question for owner:** what is the percentage-penalty formula (basis: % of arrears per day? per month? compounding or simple? applied to the original obligation or the remaining arrears?) — once supplied, this becomes a normal (COMPLEXITY: high, money-math) build task. Until then, either leave percentage as an explicitly unsupported `contributionPenaltyType` value (current behavior, correct-but-broken UX for any group misconfigured with it), or reseed/relabel this one smoketest group's rules to `'fixed'` if it was only ever meant to exercise the supported path.

## CYCLE 51 (2026-07-17) — PRODUCTION BUG reported + reproduced by owner: stale session identity survives a DB wipe. Traces to **A3** (session-auth foundation — the `require_role()`/`require_auth()` gate). This is a gap in an already-`[x]` deliverable, not a new feature; A3 stays checked, this hardens it.

- [x] **P6 — DONE cycle 51, QA PASS (first pass, no retry needed).** `current_user()` (`api/lib/session.php:43-57`) trusts `$_SESSION['uid']`/`$_SESSION['email']` from the PHP session cookie without ever confirming the uid still exists in the `users` table. PHP's session store is entirely independent of MySQL, so a session can outlive the row it names. **Reproduced live:** after an owner-authorized DB wipe+reseed (unrelated maintenance), a browser holding a pre-wipe login stayed "authenticated" — `session_status_handler`, `groups.mine`, and every `require_auth()`-gated endpoint kept accepting the dead uid and returned empty results (`groups.mine` → `{groups: []}`) instead of a 401. The user saw "You haven't joined any groups yet" plus a stale identity (wrong name/avatar initial) in the nav, with no signal anything was wrong. This is a step below E4's "no silent blank screens" bar, applied to **identity** rather than data-loading: a phantom-identity 200 masquerading as valid, in the money app's identity gate that every other check depends on.
  **Fix (isolated to `current_user()`):** verify the session uid still exists in `users` (lightweight `SELECT uid FROM users WHERE uid = :uid LIMIT 1` — `users.uid` is the PK, so this is one indexed lookup); if it does not, destroy the session and return `null` so `require_auth()`'s existing 401 path fires naturally. Do NOT touch `require_auth()`, `require_role()`, or any handler — the existing 401 contract every page already handles (`select_group_sql.js`'s `handleSessionError`) simply starts firing for this case instead of never firing. Correctness over micro-optimization: one extra PK lookup per authenticated request is cheaper than a stale-identity 200. Dispatched to backend-specialist below.

## CYCLE 52 (2026-07-17) — PRODUCTION BUG reported + reproduced by owner: mid-page 401 fallback redirects to a hardcoded absolute `/login.html` (domain root), stranding the user on a 404. Traces to **A3** (session-auth foundation — the client-side half of the same session-expiry contract P6 hardened server-side). A3 stays checked; this fixes a gap in it. **This is the direct downstream sibling of P6:** now that the server correctly kills a stale session and returns 401 (cycle 51), the client-side 401 handler that fires next redirects to the wrong URL.

- [x] **P7 — DONE cycle 52, QA PASS (first pass, no retry needed).** 21 occurrences across 20 `scripts/*_sql.js` files call `window.location.replace("/login.html")` with a hardcoded ABSOLUTE path (domain root) as their local 401-handling fallback (inside each page's `handleApiError`/`handleSessionError`, distinct from the initial `requireSession()` gate). The app is deployed at `https://promanaged-it.com/bank_nkhonde/`, NOT the domain root, so this redirect lands on a 404 / unrelated root site instead of the real login page whenever a session expires or is rejected mid-page. `scripts/api.js` already computes this correctly: `LOGIN_URL = BASE_PATH + "login.html"` (line 28, `BASE_PATH` auto-detected from `import.meta.url`, deployment-agnostic) and its own `requireSession()` (line 196) uses `window.location.replace(LOGIN_URL)`. Proof this is a bug not a design choice: `admin_dashboard_sql.js:1442` uses a correct *relative* `"../login.html"` for its logout handler while line 1455 in the SAME FILE uses the broken absolute path for its 401 case.
  **Fix (additive helper + 20 mechanical call-site swaps):** add `export function redirectToLogin() { window.location.replace(LOGIN_URL); }` to `scripts/api.js` next to `LOGIN_URL` (line 28) — done first as a tiny prerequisite so both parallel batches only consume it, keeping their file lists disjoint from api.js and from each other. Then in each of the 20 files, add `redirectToLogin` to the EXISTING `./api.js` import line (all 20 already import `apiGet`/`apiPost`/etc. from it — one more named import, not a new statement) and replace the bare `window.location.replace("/login.html")` with `redirectToLogin()`. **Only the literal `"/login.html"` absolute occurrences are in scope** — do NOT touch the `../login.html` relative redirects (logout handlers etc.), which are already correct. COMPLEXITY: not high — no logic change, no money math, purely mechanical. Grep-verified file:line list (fresh this session):
  - Batch A (10 files): admin_dashboard_sql.js:1455, analytics_sql.js:492, approve_registrations_sql.js:353, broadcast_notifications_sql.js:277, complete_profile_sql.js:174, contacts_sql.js:470, contributions_overview_sql.js:564, financial_reports_sql.js:498, interest_penalties_sql.js:581, invitation_code_sql.js:315.
  - Batch B (10 files): loan_payments_sql.js:600, manage_loans_sql.js:1445, manage_members_new_sql.js:793, manage_payments_sql.js:1119, manage_rules_sql.js:344, messages_sql.js:571, seed_money_overview_sql.js:324, settings_sql.js:237 AND :324 (two occurrences — swap both), select_group_sql.js:260, user_analytics_sql.js:552, user_dashboard_sql.js:1053.
  Dispatched below: prerequisite (api.js export) first, then Batch A + Batch B frontend-specialists in parallel (disjoint file lists, neither touches api.js).

## CYCLE 54 (2026-07-17) — ROOT CAUSE FOUND for the entire "no groups"/"stale identity" symptom chased across cycles 51-53. `scripts/api.js`'s `request()` returned the FULL server envelope (`{ok:true, data:{...}}`, per `api/lib/http.php:json_response()`) unchanged instead of unwrapping to `data`. Every caller in the app (`listMyGroups()` reading `.groups`, every `_sql.js` page reading `.members`/`.loans`/`.rules`/`.email`) accesses fields directly off the result assuming it was already unwrapped — it never was, in any commit. **Proven empirically**, not theorized: simulated the exact old client logic in Node against the live production API — `loginResult.email` and `groups.groups` both came back `undefined` with the old code, both resolve correctly with the fix. This is very likely the TRUE original root cause the owner kept hitting (the "Member"/"M" fallback avatar in the very first screenshot this session matches exactly — session.email undefined → falls back to the literal string "Member").
- [x] **P8 — DONE cycle 54, QA PASS (first pass).** Fixed `request()`'s final return in `scripts/api.js` (was `return body;`, now unwraps to `body.data` on the success path only — error paths throw before reaching this line, `messageFrom()` still reads the full `{ok:false,error}` envelope unaffected). QA grepped the whole codebase for any double-unwrap compensation (`.data.data`, `response.data.ok`, etc.) that could have been quietly working around the old bug and would break under the fix — found none. Confirmed the 5 raw-`fetch()` file-upload call sites (settings_sql.js, loan_payments_sql.js, manage_payments_sql.js, manage_rules_sql.js, complete_profile_sql.js) bypass `request()` entirely and are unaffected. Sampled 6+ files across money/admin/member pages — all their existing `.members`/`.loans`/`.groups`/`.rules` access patterns are now correct given the fix, no further changes needed elsewhere. `git diff --stat`: only scripts/api.js changed (4 insertions, 1 deletion).

             CONTRAST — text <4.5:1 (or <3:1 for >=24px/bold) on those same 3 pages; give the computed ratio; note if none.
             NEEDS LIVE VISUAL CONFIRMATION — anything runtime/content-width dependent that cannot be statically decided.
           Each finding = exact file:line + one-clause description. If a bar has zero findings, state "none" explicitly rather than omitting it. No file changed (confirm read-only).
```

---

## What this project is

A village-banking / savings-group ("Bank Nkhonde") web app. Groups of members contribute seed money, take loans, repay with interest, and incur penalties. Two admin tiers (`admin`, `senior_admin`) manage members, loans, payments, rules, and reports; members see their own dashboard, contributions, and loans.

**Stack:** static HTML + vanilla ES-module JS + plain CSS, served by Firebase Hosting. Firebase Auth + Firestore + Storage. One Cloud Functions file (`functions/index.js`, Node 18, CommonJS, nodemailer) for email. **No build step. No bundler. No test framework.** `config.php` is a vestigial leftover with no PHP runtime deployed.

---
